1\n```\n\n### Compound Growth\n```\n=Starting_Value * (1 + Growth_Rate)^Years\n```\n\n### Year-over-Year Growth\n```\n=(Value_t / Value_t-1) - 1\n```\n\n## Economic Formulas\n\n### Depreciation Rate\n```\n=Consumption_of_Fixed_Capital / Capital_Stock\n```\n\n### Capital-Output Ratio\n```\n=Capital_Stock / GDP\n```\n\n### Cobb-Douglas Production Function\n```\nY = A * K^α * L^(1-α)\n```\nIn Excel:\n```\n=EXP(LnA) * (K^alpha) * (L^(1-alpha))\n```\n\n## Best Practices\n\n1. **Use Absolute References ($)** when you want to lock cell references\n   - `$A$1` - locks both row and column\n   - `$A1` - locks only column\n   - `A$1` - locks only row\n\n2. **Named Ranges** for clarity\n   - Define names for important cells/ranges\n   - Use in formulas for readability\n\n3. **Error Handling**\n   - Use IFERROR to handle potential errors\n   ```\n   =IFERROR(A1/B1, 0)\n   ```\n\n4. **Documentation**\n   - Add comments to complex formulas\n   - Use adjacent cells for formula explanations\n\n## Linking Data Between Sheets\n\n```\n=SheetName!A1\n='Sheet Name With Spaces'!A1\n```\n\n## Dynamic References\n\nUse INDEX/MATCH for flexible lookups:\n```\n=INDEX(return_range, MATCH(lookup_value, lookup_range, 0))\n```\n"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":5,"cache_creation_input_tokens":203,"cache_read_input_tokens":19395,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":203,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"output_tokens":1},"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"bd6c30d6-46a3-48ab-8769-de62b70cfa43","uuid":"5c2d4ab7-36b0-45f2-87ed-464ade0f4176"}
{"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-sonnet-4-5-20250929","id":"msg_vrtx_019uo1tzym9Yu6LR2aaDd3jU","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_vrtx_011Lp8ZjuwFvnrJV2zMxAJgM","name":"Write","input":{"file_path":"/root/environment/skills/excel-solver-optimization.md","content":"# Excel Solver for Optimization\n\n## Overview\nExcel Solver is an add-in tool for optimization problems. It's commonly used in economics for:\n- HP (Hodrick-Prescott) filtering\n- Parameter estimation\n- Constraint optimization\n- Finding optimal solutions\n\n## Enabling Solver\n\n### In Excel for Windows\n1. File → Options → Add-ins\n2. Manage: Excel Add-ins → Go\n3. Check \"Solver Add-in\" → OK\n\n### In Excel for Mac\n1. Tools → Excel Add-ins\n2. Check \"Solver Add-in\" → OK\n\n## Basic Solver Setup\n\n### Components\n1. **Objective Cell**: The cell to minimize/maximize\n2. **Variable Cells**: Cells that Solver can change\n3. **Constraints**: Limits on variable cells (optional)\n\n### Accessing Solver\n- Data tab → Analyze group → Solver\n\n## HP Filter Implementation in Excel\n\n### What is HP Filter?\nThe Hodrick-Prescott filter separates a time series into:\n- **Trend component**: Smooth, long-term movement\n- **Cyclical component**: Short-term fluctuations\n\n### HP Filter Formula\nMinimize:\n```\nΣ(Y_t - Trend_t)² + λ * Σ(ΔTrend_t - ΔTrend_t-1)²\n```\n\nWhere:\n- Y_t = actual series\n- Trend_t = smoothed trend\n- λ = smoothing parameter (typically 100 for annual data, 1600 for quarterly)\n- Δ = first difference operator\n\n### Excel Implementation Steps\n\n#### Step 1: Set up columns\n```\n| Year | Y_t | Trend | Cycle | 1st Diff | 2nd Diff |\n```\n\n#### Step 2: Initial Trend Values\nStart with Trend = Y (as initial guess)\n\n#### Step 3: Calculate Components\n- Cycle: `=Y_t - Trend`\n- 1st Difference: `=Trend_t - Trend_t-1`\n- 2nd Difference: `=1st_Diff_t - 1st_Diff_t-1`\n\n#### Step 4: Objective Function\n```\n=SUMPRODUCT(Cycle_Range, Cycle_Range) +\n Lambda * SUMPRODUCT(2nd_Diff_Range, 2nd_Diff_Range)\n```\n\n#### Step 5: Run Solver\n- Set Objective: Objective cell\n- To: Min\n- By Changing: Trend column cells\n- Solving Method: GRG Nonlinear\n\n### Example Setup for Annual Data\n\n```\nA: Years (2000-2023)\nB: LnY (actual log values)\nC: LnY_HP (trend - variable cells)\nD: Cycle (=B-C)\nE: 1st Diff (=C2-C1, starting from row 2)\nF: 2nd Diff (=E3-E2, starting from row 3)\n\nObjective Cell:\n=SUMPRODUCT(D:D, D:D) + 100 * SUMPRODUCT(F:F, F:F)\n```\n\n### Solver Settings for HP Filter\n\n**Set Objective**: Cell with objective function\n**To**: Min\n**By Changing Variable Cells**: Trend column (e.g., C2:C24)\n**Solving Method**: GRG Nonlinear\n**Constraints**: None typically needed\n\n**Options to adjust**:\n- Precision: 0.000001\n- Constraint Precision: 0.000001\n- Max Iterations: 1000\n\n## Common Issues and Solutions\n\n### Solver Not Converging\n- Increase max iterations\n- Adjust precision settings\n- Check for circular references\n- Ensure initial values are reasonable\n\n### Results Seem Wrong\n- Verify objective function formula\n- Check that all cell references are correct\n- Ensure no absolute references where relative needed\n- Verify lambda parameter value\n\n### Solver Runs Slowly\n- Reduce number of variable cells if possible\n- Use better initial guess\n- Simplify objective function if possible\n\n## Alternative: Using Array Formulas\n\nFor some optimization problems, you can use matrix operations:\n```\n=MMULT(TRANSPOSE(X), X)\n=MINVERSE(A)\n```\n\n## Best Practices\n\n1. **Document your setup**: Add notes about what each component does\n2. **Save before running**: Solver modifies cells\n3. **Test with small data**: Verify logic before full dataset\n4. **Keep backups**: Save versions before major solver runs\n5. **Validate results**: Check that trends make economic sense\n\n## HP Filter Smoothing Parameters\n\n| Frequency | Typical λ |\n|-----------|-----------|\n| Annual    | 100       |\n| Quarterly | 1600      |\n| Monthly   | 14,400    |\n\n## References\n\n- Hodrick, R. J., & Prescott, E. C. (1997). Postwar US business cycles: an empirical investigation\n- Excel Solver uses GRG (Generalized Reduced Gradient) algorithm for nonlinear problems\n"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":5,"cache_creation_input_tokens":203,"cache_read_input_tokens":19395,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":203,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"output_tokens":1},"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"bd6c30d6-46a3-48ab-8769-de62b70cfa43","uuid":"5700721f-d0c9-44a1-b58f-ef414417d11f"}

Click dropdown to open\n2. Locate option elements\n3. Click desired option\n4. Check for consistency\n\n## Integration with Excel\n\nOnce data is extracted:\n1. Structure in appropriate format (year, value pairs)\n2. Write directly to Excel cells\n3. Economic Data Sources and APIs\n\n## Overview\nThis skill covers major economic data sources, their structure, and how to access them for macroeconomic modeling.\n\n## Penn World Table (PWT)\n\n### Overview\n- Comprehensive dataset for international comparisons\n- Covers 183 countries from 1950-2019 (version 10.01)\n- Provides GDP, capital stock, employment, and productivity measures\n\n### URL\n```\nhttps://www.rug.nl/ggdc/productivity/pwt/\n```\n\n### Key Variables\n\n#### Capital Stock\n- `ck`: Capital stock at current PPPs (millions 2017 USD)\n- `cn`: Capital stock at constant national prices\n- `rnna`: Real national income\n\n#### GDP Measures\n- `rgdpe`: Expenditure-side real GDP at chained PPPs\n- `rgdpo`: Output-side real GDP at chained PPPs\n- `cgdpe`: Expenditure-side real GDP at current PPPs\n\n#### Labor\n- `emp`: Number of persons engaged (millions)\n- `avh`: Average annual hours worked by persons engaged\n\n#### Productivity\n- `rtfpna`: TFP at constant national prices (USA=1)\n- `labsh`: Share of labor compensation in GDP\n\n### File Format\n- Excel (.xlsx)\n- CSV\n- Stata (.dta)\n\n### Metadata\nEach PWT release includes metadata sheet explaining:\n- Variable definitions\n- Calculation methods\n- Data sources\n- Coverage notes\n\n## IMF World Economic Outlook (WEO)\n\n### Overview\n- Published twice yearly (April and October)\n- Covers ~190 countries\n- Historical data and forecasts\n\n### URL\n```\nhttps://www.imf.org/en/Publications/WEO/weo-database\n```\n\n### Key Indicators for Georgia\n\n#### National Accounts\n- GDP (constant prices, billions)\n- GDP growth rate (%)\n- GDP per capita (constant prices)\n- GDP based on PPP\n\n#### Prices\n- Inflation (average consumer prices, %)\n- GDP deflator (% change)\n\n#### External Sector\n- Current account balance (% of GDP)\n- Exports/Imports of goods and services\n\n#### Fiscal\n- General government revenue/expenditure\n- General government gross debt (% of GDP)\n\n### Data Access\n1. **Interactive Database**: Select country and indicators\n2. **Download Entire Database**: Excel or CSV format\n3. **WEO Reports**: PDF with tables\n\n### Time Coverage\n- Historical: Usually from 1980\n- Projections: 5 years ahead\n\n## European Central Bank (ECB) Data\n\n### Overview\n- Statistical Data Warehouse (SDW)\n- Covers EU and neighboring countries\n- Detailed national accounts data\n\n### URL for Georgia\n```\nhttps://data.ecb.europa.eu/data/geographical-areas/georgia\n```\n\n### Key Datasets\n\n#### National Accounts\n- GDP and components\n- **Consumption of Fixed Capital** (CFC)\n  - Annual data\n  - In national currency or USD\n  - Essential for depreciation calculations\n\n#### External Statistics\n- Balance of payments\n- International investment position\n\n### Data Format\n- Excel download\n- CSV\n- SDMX (Statistical Data and Metadata eXchange)\n\n### Accessing CFC Data\n1. Navigate to Georgia page\n2. Select \"National Accounts\"\n3. Find \"Consumption of Fixed Capital\"\n4. Select annual frequency\n5. Download data\n\n## World Bank Data\n\n### Overview\n- World Development Indicators (WDI)\n- Extensive coverage, easy API access\n\n### Key Indicators\n- `NY.GDP.MKTP.KD`: GDP (constant 2015 USD)\n- `NY.GDP.MKTP.KD.ZG`: GDP growth (%)\n- `NE.GDI.FTOT.KD`: Gross fixed capital formation\n- `NY.GDP.DEFL.KD.ZG`: GDP deflator\n\n### API Access\n```\nhttps://api.worldbank.org/v2/country/GEO/indicator/NY.GDP.MKTP.KD?format=json\n```\n\n## Data Integration Best Practices\n\n### Currency Consistency\n- Use consistent currency (USD or local)\n- Apply exchange rates if needed\n- Note PPP vs market exchange rates\n\n### Time Period Alignment\n- Match fiscal years vs calendar years\n- Handle data lags (latest available data)\n- Align frequencies (annual, quarterly)\n\n### Data Quality Checks\n\n#### Consistency\n- Check for breaks in series\n- Verify growth rates match levels\n- Cross-reference between sources\n\n#### Coverage\n- Note missing years\n- Understand estimation methods\n- Check for revisions\n\n### Handling Missing Data\n\n#### Interpolation\n- Linear interpolation for short gaps\n- Trend-based for longer gaps\n\n#### Extrapolation\n- Use growth rates from similar countries\n- Apply regional averages\n- Use model-based forecasts\n\n## Georgia-Specific Considerations\n\n### Statistical Agency\n- National Statistics Office of Georgia (Geostat)\n- URL: `https://www.geostat.ge/en`\n\n### Data Availability\n- Good coverage from 1995 onwards\n- Earlier data may be less reliable\n- Recent years updated regularly\n\n### Economic Context\n- Small open economy\n- Transition economy (post-Soviet)\n- Significant remittances impact\n- Tourism-dependent sectors\n\n## Depreciation Rate Calculation\n\n### Using CFC and Capital Stock\n\n```\nDepreciation Rate (δ) = CFC / Capital Stock\n```\n\nWhere:\n- CFC: Consumption of Fixed Capital (annual)\n- Capital Stock: Total capital stock\n\n### Data Sources\n- CFC: ECB or Geostat\n- Capital Stock: PWT (ck or cn variables)\n\n### Typical Range\n- Annual depreciation: 4-8% for most countries\n- Higher for developing economies\n- {"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-sonnet-4-5-20250929","id":"msg_vrtx_019uo1tzym9Yu6LR2aaDd3jU","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_vrtx_014My6xXCeEdTkpiBWXnkvHA","name":"Write","input":{"file_path":"/root/environment/skills/cobb-douglas-production-function.md","content":"# Cobb-Douglas Production Function for Macroeconomic Modeling\n\n## Overview\nThe Cobb-Douglas production function is fundamental in growth economics and potential output estimation.\n\n## Basic Form\n\n### Standard Specification\n```\nY = A * K^α * L^(1-α)\n```\n\nWhere:\n- **Y**: Output (GDP)\n- **A**: Total Factor Productivity (TFP) or technology\n- **K**: Capital stock\n- **L**: Labor input\n- **α**: Capital's share of income (typically 0.3-0.4)\n\n### Intensive Form (Per Worker)\n```\ny = A * k^α\n```\n\nWhere:\n- y = Y/L (output per worker)\n- k = K/L (capital per worker)\n\n## Log-Linear Transformation\n\nTaking natural logarithm:\n```\nln(Y) = ln(A) + α*ln(K) + (1-α)*ln(L)\n```\n\nUseful for:\n- Decomposition analysis\n- Growth accounting\n- Trend extraction\n\n## Parameters\n\n### Capital Share (α)\n\n**Typical Values**:\n- Advanced economies: 0.33-0.36\n- Developing economies: 0.35-0.40\n- Can estimate from national accounts data\n\n**Estimation from Data**:\n```\nα = (Capital Compensation) / GDP\n  = (GDP - Labor Compensation) / GDP\n```\n\nIf data unavailable, use standard value: α = 0.35\n\n### Labor Share (1-α)\n- Complement of capital share\n- Typically 0.60-0.67\n- Includes wages, salaries, benefits\n\n## Total Factor Productivity (TFP)\n\n### Solow Residual Method\n```\nln(A) = ln(Y) - α*ln(K) - (1-α)*ln(L)\n```\n\nAlso called: TFP, technology, efficiency\n\n### HP Filter for Trend\n1. Calculate ln(A) from data\n2. Apply HP filter to get smooth trend\n3. Use trend for potential output estimation\n\n## Potential GDP Estimation\n\n### Method 1: Trend TFP with Actual Inputs\n```\nY* = A_trend * K^α * L^α\n```\n\nWhere:\n- Y*: Potential GDP\n- A_trend: Smoothed TFP (from HP filter)\n\n### Method 2: Simplified for Small Open Economy\nWhen labor data is limited:\n```\nY* = (Y/K^α) * K^α = Y * (K/K_base)^α\n```\n\nOr using TFP trend:\n```\nY* = exp(ln(A_trend)) * K^α * L^(1-α)\n```\n\n## Capital Stock Dynamics\n\n### Perpetual Inventory Method\n```\nK_t = (1-δ)*K_t-1 + I_t\n```\n\nWhere:\n- δ: Depreciation rate\n- I_t: Investment in period t\n\n### Capital-Output Ratio\n```\nK/Y ratio = Capital Stock / GDP\n```\n\n**Uses**:\n- Project future capital stock\n- Anchor for long-run equilibrium\n- Cross-country comparisons\n\n**Typical Values**:\n- Advanced economies: 2.5-3.5\n- Developing economies: 1.5-2.5\n- Georgia (estimate): ~2.0-2.5\n\n### Projecting Capital Stock\n```\nK_future = Y_future * (K/Y)_anchor\n```\n\nWhere (K/Y)_anchor is average of recent years\n\n## Investment Shocks\n\n### One-Time Investment\n```\nK_2026 = (1-δ)*K_2025 + I_baseline + I_shock\n```\n\n### Multi-Year Investment Program\nFor shock lasting T years:\n```\nK_t = (1-δ)*K_t-1 + I_baseline + I_shock_t\nK_t+T = (1-δ)*K_t+T-1 + I_baseline  (after shock ends)\n```\n\n### Impact on Potential GDP\n```\nΔY* = Y*_with_shock - Y*_baseline\n     = A_trend * (K_with^α - K_base^α) * L^(1-α)\n```\n\n## Small Open Economy Considerations\n\n### Assumptions\n1. **Labor**: Often assumed fixed or growing at population rate\n2. **TFP**: Trend follows historical pattern\n3. **Capital**: Adjusts via investment (domestic + FDI)\n\n### Simplified Model\nWhen L data is poor or assumed constant:\n```\nY = A * K^α\n```\n\nOr normalize:\n```\nY = Y_base * (A/A_base) * (K/K_base)^α\n```\n\n## Excel Implementation\n\n### Step 1: Calculate Historical TFP\n```\nColumn A: Years\nColumn B: Y (GDP)\nColumn C: K (Capital Stock)\nColumn D: L (Labor, if available)\nColumn E: ln(Y)\nColumn F: ln(K)\nColumn G: ln(L) or assume constant\nColumn H: ln(A) = ln(Y) - α*ln(K) - (1-α)*ln(L)\n```\n\n### Step 2: HP Filter\nApply HP filter to ln(A) to get ln(A_trend)\n\n### Step 3: Project Capital Stock\n```\nColumn I: Investment (baseline)\nColumn J: Investment (with shock)\nColumn K: K_base = (1-δ)*K_base_t-1 + I_baseline\nColumn L: K_with = (1-δ)*K_with_t-1 + I_total\n```\n\n### Step 4: Calculate Potential GDP\n```\nColumn M: ln(A_trend) - extended using TREND()\nColumn N: Y*_base = EXP(ln(A_trend)) * K_base^α\nColumn O: Y*_with = EXP(ln(A_trend)) * K_with^α\nColumn P: Difference = Y*_with - Y*_base\nColumn Q: % Difference = (Y*_with/Y*_base - 1) * 100\n```\n\n## Calibration Tips\n\n### Choosing α\n- Use country-specific data if available\n- Otherwise: α = 0.35 (standard)\n- Sensitivity analysis: Try α = 0.30 and 0.40\n\n### Choosing δ\n- Calculate from CFC data: δ = CFC/K\n- Average over recent years (e.g., 8 years)\n- Typical range: 0.04-0.08\n\n### HP Filter λ\n- Annual data: λ = 100\n- Quarterly data: λ = 1600\n\n## Growth Accounting\n\n### Decomposition\n```\nGrowth(Y) = Growth(A) + α*Growth(K) + (1-α)*Growth(L)\n```\n\n### Contributions\n```\nContribution of K = α * [ln(K_t) - ln(K_t-1)] / [ln(Y_t) - ln(Y_t-1)]\nContribution of A = [ln(A_t) - ln(A_t-1)] / [ln(Y_t) - ln(Y_t-1)]\n```\n\n## Validation Checks\n\n### Output Gap\n```\nOutput Gap = (Y - Y*) / Y* * 100\n```\n\nShould be:\n- Near 0% on average\n- Positive in booms\n- Negative in recessions\n\n### Capital-Output Ratio\n- Should be stable over time\n- Check against international comparisons\n\n### TFP Growth\n- Typical: 0.5-2% per year\n- Negative growth signals problems\n\n## Common Issues\n\n### Capital Stock Jumps\n- Check for breaks in series\n- Adjust for revaluations\n- Ensure consistent currency\n\n### Implausible TFP\n- Verify Y and K data quality\n- Check α parameter\n- Consider measurement error\n\n### Trend Not Smooth\n- Adjust HP filter λ\n- Check for data outliers\n- Ensure sufficient time series length\n\n## Georgia Context\n\n### Typical Parameters\n- α: 0.35-0.38 (estimate)\n- δ: 0.05-0.07 (to be calculated from data)\n- K/Y ratio: ~2.0-2.5\n\n### Data Sources\n- Y: IMF WEO\n- K: Penn World Table\n- CFC: ECB or Geostat\n- L: PWT or ILO (if needed)\n\n### Investment Shock (6.5B USD over 8 years)\n- Georgia GDP ~24B USD (2025 est.)\n- Shock is ~3.4% of annual GDP\n- Significant impact expected\n- Spread over 8 years smooths effect\n\n## References\n\n- Cobb, C. W., & Douglas, P. H. (1928). A theory of production\n- Solow, R. M. (1957). Technical change and the aggregate production function\n- Hall, R. E., & Jones, C. I. (1999). Why do some countries produce so much more output per worker than others?\n"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":5,"cache_creation_input_tokens":203,"cache_read_input_tokens":19395,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":203,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"output_tokens":1},"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"bd6c30d6-46a3-48ab-8769-de62b70cfa43","uuid":"ed418ca2-1f35-4128-8e0c-a0aabf923499"}

It's commonly used in economics for:\n- HP (Hodrick-Prescott) filtering\n- Parameter estimation\n- Constraint optimization\n- Finding optimal solutions\n\n## Enabling Solver\n\n### In Excel for Windows\n1. File → Options → Add-ins\n2. Manage: Excel Add-ins → Go\n3. Check \"Solver Add-in\" → OK\n\n### In Excel for Mac\n1. Tools → Excel Add-ins\n2. Check \"Solver Add-in\" → OK\n\n## Basic Solver Setup\n\n### Components\n1. **Objective Cell**: The cell to minimize/maximize\n2. **Variable Cells**: Cells that Solver can change\n3. **Constraints**: Limits on variable cells (optional)\n\n### Accessing Solver\n- Data tab → Analyze group → Solver\n\n## HP Filter Implementation in Excel\n\n### What is HP Filter?\nThe Hodrick-Prescott filter separates a time series into:\n- **Trend component**: Smooth, long-term movement\n- **Cyclical component**: Short-term fluctuations\n\n### HP Filter Formula\nMinimize:\n```\nΣ(Y_t - Trend_t)² + λ * Σ(ΔTrend_t - ΔTrend_t-1)²\n```\n\nWhere:\n- Y_t = actual series\n- Trend_t = smoothed trend\n- λ = smoothing parameter (typically 100 for annual data, 1600 for quarterly)\n- Δ = first difference operator\n\n### Excel Implementation Steps\n\n#### Step 1: Set up columns\n```\n| Year | Y_t | Trend | Cycle | 1st Diff | 2nd Diff |\n```\n\n#### Step 2: Initial Trend Values\nStart with Trend = Y (as initial guess)\n\n#### Step 3: Calculate Components\n- Cycle: `=Y_t - Trend`\n- 1st Difference: `=Trend_t - Trend_t-1`\n- 2nd Difference: `=1st_Diff_t - 1st_Diff_t-1`\n\n#### Step 4: Objective Function\n```\n=SUMPRODUCT(Cycle_Range, Cycle_Range) +\n Lambda * SUMPRODUCT(2nd_Diff_Range, 2nd_Diff_Range)\n```\n\n#### Step 5: Run Solver\n- Set Objective: Objective cell\n- To: Min\n- By Changing: Trend column cells\n- Solving Method: GRG Nonlinear\n\n### Example Setup for Annual Data\n\n```\nA: Years (2000-2023)\nB: LnY (actual log values)\nC: LnY_HP (trend - variable cells)\nD: Cycle (=B-C)\nE: 1st Diff (=C2-C1, starting from row 2)\nF: 2nd Diff (=E3-E2, starting from row 3)\n\nObjective Cell:\n=SUMPRODUCT(D:D, D:D) + 100 * SUMPRODUCT(F:F, F:F)\n```\n\n### Solver Settings for HP Filter\n\n**Set Objective**: Cell with objective function\n**To**: Min\n**By Changing Variable Cells**: Trend column (e.g., C2:C24)\n**Solving Method**: GRG Nonlinear\n**Constraints**: None typically needed\n\n**Options to adjust**:\n- Precision: 0.000001\n- Constraint Precision: 0.000001\n- Max Iterations: 1000\n\n## Common Issues and Solutions\n\n### Solver Not Converging\n- Increase max iterations\n- Adjust precision settings\n- Check for circular references\n- Ensure initial values are reasonable\n\n### Results Seem Wrong\n- Verify objective function formula\n- Check that all cell references are correct\n- Ensure no absolute references where relative needed\n- Verify lambda parameter value\n\n### Solver Runs Slowly\n- Reduce number of variable cells if possible\n- Use better initial guess\n- Simplify objective function if possible\n\n## Alternative: Using Array Formulas\n\nFor some optimization problems, you can use matrix operations:\n```\n=MMULT(TRANSPOSE(X), X)\n=MINVERSE(A)\n```\n\n## Best Practices\n\n1. **Document your setup**: Add notes about what each component does\n2. **Save before running**: Solver modifies cells\n3. **Test with small data**: Verify logic before full dataset\n4. **Keep backups**: Save versions before major solver runs\n5. **Validate results**: Check that trends make economic sense\n\n## HP Filter Smoothing Parameters\n\n| Frequency | Typical λ |\n|-----------|-----------|\n| Annual    | 100       |\n| Quarterly | 1600      |\n| Monthly   | 14,400    |\n\n## References\n\n- Hodrick, R. J., & Prescott, E. C. (1997). Postwar US business cycles: an empirical investigation\n- Excel Solver uses GRG (Generalized Reduced Gradient) algorithm for nonlinear problems\n","structuredPatch":[],"originalFile":null}}

Economic Data Sources and APIs\n\n## Overview\nThis skill covers major economic data sources, their structure, and how to access them for macroeconomic modeling.\n\n## Penn World Table (PWT)\n\n### Overview\n- Comprehensive dataset for international comparisons\n- Covers 183 countries from 1950-2019 (version 10.01)\n- Provides GDP, capital stock, employment, and productivity measures\n\n### URL\n```\nhttps://www.rug.nl/ggdc/productivity/pwt/\n```\n\n### Key Variables\n\n#### Capital Stock\n- `ck`: Capital stock at current PPPs (millions 2017 USD)\n- `cn`: Capital stock at constant Covers ~190 countries\n- Historical data and forecasts\n\n### URL\n```\nhttps://www.imf.org/en/Publications/WEO/weo-database\n```\n\n### Key Indicators for Georgia\n\n#### National Accounts\n- GDP Historical: Usually from 1980\n- Projections: 5 years ahead\n\n## European Central Bank (ECB) Data\n\n### Overview\n- Statistical Data Warehouse (SDW)\n- Covers EU and neighboring countries\n- frequencies (annual, quarterly)\n\n### Data Quality Checks\n\n#### Consistency\n- Check for breaks in series\n- Verify growth rates match levels\n- Cross-reference between sources\n\n#### Coverage\n- Note missing years\n- Understand estimation methods\n- Check for revisions\n\n### Handling Missing Data\n\n#### Interpolation\n- Linear interpolation for short gaps\n- Trend-based for longer gaps\n\n#### Extrapolation\n- Use growth rates from similar countries\n- Apply regional averages\n- Use model-based forecasts\n\n## Georgia-Specific Considerations\n\n### Statistical Agency\n- stock\n\n### Data Sources\n- CFC: ECB or Geostat\n- Capital Stock: PWT (ck or cn variables)\n\n### Typical Range\n- Annual depreciation: 4-8% for most countries\n- Higher for developing economies\n- {"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_vrtx_014My6xXCeEdTkpiBWXnkvHA","type":"tool_result","content":"File created successfully at: /root/environment/skills/cobb-douglas-production-function.md"}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"bd6c30d6-46a3-48ab-8769-de62b70cfa43","uuid":"6d9bfbfa-cb8c-4259-b791-320e25bb8558","tool_use_result":{"type":"create","filePath":"/root/environment/skills/cobb-douglas-production-function.md","content":"# Cobb-Douglas Production Function for Macroeconomic Modeling\n\n## Overview\nThe Cobb-Douglas production function is fundamental in growth economics and potential output estimation.\n\n## Basic Form\n\n### Standard Specification\n```\nY = A * K^α * L^(1-α)\n```\n\nWhere:\n- **Y**: Output (GDP)\n- **A**: Total Factor Productivity (TFP) or technology\n- **K**: Capital stock\n- **L**: Labor input\n- **α**: Capital's share of income (typically 0.3-0.4)\n\n### Intensive Form (Per Worker)\n```\ny = A * k^α\n```\n\nWhere:\n- y = Y/L (output per worker)\n- k = K/L (capital per worker)\n\n## Log-Linear Transformation\n\nTaking natural logarithm:\n```\nln(Y) = ln(A) + α*ln(K) + (1-α)*ln(L)\n```\n\nUseful for:\n- Decomposition analysis\n- Growth accounting\n- Trend extraction\n\n## Parameters\n\n### Capital Share (α)\n\n**Typical Values**:\n- Advanced economies: 0.33-0.36\n- Developing economies: 0.35-0.40\n- Can estimate from national accounts data\n\n**Estimation from Data**:\n```\nα = (Capital Compensation) / GDP\n  = (GDP - Labor Compensation) / GDP\n```\n\nIf data unavailable, use standard value: α = 0.35\n\n### Labor Share (1-α)\n- Complement of capital share\n- Typically 0.60-0.67\n- Includes wages, salaries, benefits\n\n## Total Factor Productivity (TFP)\n\n### Solow Residual Method\n```\nln(A) = ln(Y) - α*ln(K) - (1-α)*ln(L)\n```\n\nAlso called: TFP, technology, efficiency\n\n### HP Filter for Trend\n1. Calculate ln(A) from data\n2. Apply HP filter to get smooth trend\n3. Use trend for potential output estimation\n\n## Potential GDP Estimation\n\n### Method 1: Trend TFP with Actual Inputs\n```\nY* = A_trend * K^α * L^α\n```\n\nWhere:\n- Y*: Potential GDP\n- A_trend: Smoothed TFP (from HP filter)\n\n### Method 2: Simplified for Small Open Economy\nWhen labor data is limited:\n```\nY* = (Y/K^α) * K^α = Y * (K/K_base)^α\n```\n\nOr using TFP trend:\n```\nY* = exp(ln(A_trend)) * K^α * L^(1-α)\n```\n\n## Capital Stock Dynamics\n\n### Perpetual Inventory Method\n```\nK_t = (1-δ)*K_t-1 + I_t\n```\n\nWhere:\n- δ: Depreciation rate\n- I_t: Investment in period t\n\n### Capital-Output Ratio\n```\nK/Y ratio = Capital Stock / GDP\n```\n\n**Uses**:\n- Project future capital stock\n- Anchor for long-run equilibrium\n- Cross-country comparisons\n\n**Typical Values**:\n- Advanced economies: 2.5-3.5\n- Developing economies: 1.5-2.5\n- Georgia (estimate): ~2.0-2.5\n\n### Projecting Capital Stock\n```\nK_future = Y_future * (K/Y)_anchor\n```\n\nWhere (K/Y)_anchor is average of recent years\n\n## Investment Shocks\n\n### One-Time Investment\n```\nK_2026 = (1-δ)*K_2025 + I_baseline + I_shock\n```\n\n### Multi-Year Investment Program\nFor shock lasting T years:\n```\nK_t = (1-δ)*K_t-1 + I_baseline + I_shock_t\nK_t+T = (1-δ)*K_t+T-1 + I_baseline  (after shock ends)\n```\n\n### Impact on Potential GDP\n```\nΔY* = Y*_with_shock - Y*_baseline\n     = A_trend * (K_with^α - K_base^α) * L^(1-α)\n```\n\n## Small Open Economy Considerations\n\n### Assumptions\n1. **Labor**: Often assumed fixed or growing at population rate\n2. **TFP**: Trend follows historical pattern\n3. **Capital**: Adjusts via investment (domestic + FDI)\n\n### Simplified Model\nWhen L data is poor or assumed constant:\n```\nY = A * K^α\n```\n\nOr normalize:\n```\nY = Y_base * (A/A_base) * (K/K_base)^α\n```\n\n## Excel Implementation\n\n### Step 1: Calculate Historical TFP\n```\nColumn A: Years\nColumn B: Y (GDP)\nColumn C: K (Capital Stock)\nColumn D: L (Labor, if available)\nColumn E: ln(Y)\nColumn F: ln(K)\nColumn G: ln(L) or assume constant\nColumn H: ln(A) = ln(Y) - α*ln(K) - (1-α)*ln(L)\n```\n\n### Step 2: HP Filter\nApply HP filter to ln(A) to get ln(A_trend)\n\n### Step 3: Project Capital Stock\n```\nColumn I: Investment (baseline)\nColumn J: Investment (with shock)\nColumn K: K_base = (1-δ)*K_base_t-1 + I_baseline\nColumn L: K_with = (1-δ)*K_with_t-1 + I_total\n```\n\n### Step 4: Calculate Potential GDP\n```\nColumn M: ln(A_trend) - extended using TREND()\nColumn N: Y*_base = EXP(ln(A_trend)) * K_base^α\nColumn O: Y*_with = EXP(ln(A_trend)) * K_with^α\nColumn P: Difference = Y*_with - Y*_base\nColumn Q: % Difference = (Y*_with/Y*_base - 1) * 100\n```\n\n## Calibration Tips\n\n### Choosing α\n- Use country-specific data if available\n- Otherwise: α = 0.35 (standard)\n- Sensitivity analysis: Try α = 0.30 and 0.40\n\n### Choosing δ\n- Calculate from CFC data: δ = CFC/K\n- Average over recent years (e.g., 8 years)\n- Typical range: 0.04-0.08\n\n### HP Filter λ\n- Annual data: λ = 100\n- Quarterly data: λ = 1600\n\n## Growth Accounting\n\n### Decomposition\n```\nGrowth(Y) = Growth(A) + α*Growth(K) + (1-α)*Growth(L)\n```\n\n### Contributions\n```\nContribution of K = α * [ln(K_t) - ln(K_t-1)] / [ln(Y_t) - ln(Y_t-1)]\nContribution of A = [ln(A_t) - ln(A_t-1)] / [ln(Y_t) - ln(Y_t-1)]\n```\n\n## Validation Checks\n\n### Output Gap\n```\nOutput Gap = (Y - Y*) / Y* * 100\n```\n\nShould be:\n- Near 0% on average\n- Positive in booms\n- Negative in recessions\n\n### Capital-Output Ratio\n- Should be stable over time\n- Check against international comparisons\n\n### TFP Growth\n- Typical: 0.5-2% per year\n- Negative growth signals problems\n\n## Common Issues\n\n### Capital Stock Jumps\n- Check for breaks in series\n- Adjust for revaluations\n- Ensure consistent currency\n\n### Implausible TFP\n- Verify Y and K data quality\n- Check α parameter\n- Consider measurement error\n\n### Trend Not Smooth\n- Adjust HP filter λ\n- Check for data outliers\n- Ensure sufficient time series length\n\n## Georgia Context\n\n### Typical Parameters\n- α: 0.35-0.38 (estimate)\n- δ: 0.05-0.07 (to be calculated from data)\n- K/Y ratio: ~2.0-2.5\n\n### Data Sources\n- Y: IMF WEO\n- K: Penn World Table\n- CFC: ECB or Geostat\n- L: PWT or ILO (if needed)\n\n### Investment Shock (6.5B USD over 8 years)\n- Georgia GDP ~24B USD (2025 est.)\n- Shock is ~3.4% of annual GDP\n- Significant impact expected\n- Spread over 8 years smooths effect\n\n## References\n\n- Cobb, C. W., & Douglas, P. H. (1928). A theory of production\n- Solow, R. M. (1957). Technical change and the aggregate production function\n- Hall, R. E., & Jones, C. I. (1999). Why do some countries produce so much more output per worker than others?\n","structuredPatch":[],"originalFile":null}}

(1-delta)*K_prev = Y*K/Y - K_prev + delta*K_prev\n        ws[f'D{row}'] = f'=(1-$B$3)*D{row-1} + (B{row}*$B$4 - (1-$B$3)*D{row-1})'\n    \n    # LnZ_trend\n    if year <= 2023:\n        # Link to HP filtered values\n        ws[f'E{row}'] = f'=H{hist_row}'\n    else:\n        # Extend trend using TREND function\n        # TREND(known_y's, known_x's, new_x)\n        ws[f'E{row}'] = f'=TREND($E$36:$E$57,ROW($E$36:$E$57),ROW())'\n    \n    # Ystar_base = exp(LnZ_trend) * K_base^alpha\n    ws[f'F{row}'] = f'=EXP(E{row})*POWER(D{row},$B$2)'\n    \n    # Investment shock from Investment sheet\n    inv_row = 2 + (year - 2024)  # Investment sheet starts at row 2 with 2024\n    if year >= 2024:\n        ws[f'G{row}'] = f'=Investment!B{inv_row}'\n    else:\n        ws[f'G{row}'] = 0\n    \n    # deltaK_shock (cumulative)\n    if year == 2002:\n        ws[f'H{row}'] = 0\n    else:\n        ws[f'H{row}'] = f'=(1-$B$3)*H{row-1} + G{row}'\n    \n    # K_with = K_base + deltaK_shock\n    ws[f'I{row}'] = f'=D{row}+H{row}'\n    \n    # Ystar_with = exp(LnZ_trend) * K_with^alpha\n    ws[f'J{row}'] = f'=EXP(E{row})*POWER(I{row},$B$2)'\n    \n    # Delta_Y = Ystar_with - Ystar_base\n    ws[f'K{row}'] = f'=J{row}-F{row}'\n    \n    # Delta_Y_pct = Delta_Y / Ystar_base * 100\n    ws[f'L{row}'] = f'=K{row}/F{row}*100'\n    \n    row += 1\n\nprint(f\"Production function projections set up for years 2002-2041\")\n\n# Update K/Y anchor formula now that we know the range\n# Average K/Y of last 9 years (2015-2023): rows 22-30 in the projection section\nanchor_start = data_start + (2015 - 2002)\nanchor_end = data_start + (2023 - 2002)\nws['B4'] = f'=AVERAGE(C{anchor_start}:C{anchor_end})'\n\nprint(f\"K/Y anchor formula: =AVERAGE(C{anchor_start}:C{anchor_end})\")\n\n# Save\nwb.save('/root/test-supply.xlsx')\nprint(\"Saved Production sheet with projection formulas\")\nPYEOF","description":"Set up production function projections"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":0,"cache_creation_input_tokens":1589,"cache_read_input_tokens":54133,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":1589,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"output_tokens":1},"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"bd6c30d6-46a3-48ab-8769-de62b70cfa43","uuid":"f949b29f-4d6e-437f-9388-d24ccc42d67b"}

Penn World Table (PWT) 10.0\n- **Source**: https://www.rug.nl/ggdc/productivity/pwt/\n- **Data collected**: Capital stock (cn) and depreciation rates (delta) for Georgia (2000-2019)\n- **Location in file**: `PWT` sheet, columns B-C\n\n### 2. World Bank GDP Data\n- **Source**: World Bank API (NY.GDP.MKTP.KD indicator)\n- **Data collected**: Real GDP in constant 2015 USD (2000-2024)\n- **Location in file**: `WEO_Data` sheet, column B (rows 2-26)\n\n### 3. IMF WEO Forecasts\n- **Source**: Web research of IMF projections\n- **Data collected**:\n  - 2025 growth: 7.2%\n  - 2026 growth: 5.3%\n  - 2027+ growth: 5.0% (maintained constant)\n- **Location in file**: `WEO_Data` sheet, column C (rows 27-45)\n\n### 4. ECB Consumption of Fixed Capital\n- **Source**: ECB Statistical Data Warehouse API\n- **Data collected**: CFC for Georgia in millions GEL (2000-2023)\n- **Note**: Depreciation rates use PWT delta values for consistency\n- **Location in file**: `CFC data` sheet\n\n## Excel File Structure\n\n### Sheets Created:\n\n1. **Instructions** - Overview and step-by-step guide for using the model\n2. **PWT** - Penn World Table data (capital stock, depreciation)\n3. **WEO_Data** - GDP levels and growth rates with projections to 2043\n4. **CFC data** - Consumption of Fixed Capital data\n5. **Investment** - Investment shock schedule ($0.8125B/year, 2026-2033)\n6. **Production** - Main model with HP filter and projections\n\n## Key Model Components\n\n### Parameters (Production sheet, cells B2-B4):\n- **Capital's share (α)**: 0.35 (standard value)\n- **Depreciation rate (δ)**: Calculated as average of 2016-2023 PWT deltas\n- **K/Y anchor**: Average capital-output ratio of 2015-2023\n\n### HP Filter Setup (Production sheet):\n- **Objective cell**: N10 (to minimize)\n- **Variable cells**: H9:H27 (LnZ_HP trend values)\n- **Lambda**: 100 (standard for annual data)\n- **Method**: GRG Nonlinear\n\n### Production Function:\n```\nY* = exp(LnZ_trend) × K^α\n```\n\nWhere:\n- Y* = Potential GDP\n- LnZ_trend = HP-filtered total factor productivity (in logs)\n- K = Capital stock\n- α = Capital's share (0.35)\n\n### Capital Accumulation:\n```\nK_t = (1-δ) × K_{t-1} + I_t\n```\n\nWhere:\n- δ = Depreciation rate\n- I_t = Investment (baseline + shock)\n\n## Formula Implementation\n\nAll calculations use Excel formulas (NO hardcoded values in calculation cells):\n\n### Key Formulas:\n\n1. **GDP Projection** (WEO_Data, B28-B45):\n   - `=B{t-1}*(1+C{t}/100)` - Compounds growth rates\n\n2. **TFP Calculation** (Production, G9-G27):\n   - `=LnY - α×LnK` - Solow residual\n\n3. **Baseline Capital** (Production, D37-D75):\n   - Historical: Linked from PWT\n   - Projected: `=(1-δ)×K_{t-1} + (Y_t×(K/Y)_anchor - (1-δ)×K_{t-1})`\n\n4. **Potential GDP** (Production, F36-F75):\n   - `=EXP(LnZ_trend)×K^α`\n\n5. **Impact Calculation** (Production, K36-L75):\n   - Absolute: `=Y*_with - Y*_base`\n   - Percentage: `=(Y*_with/Y*_base - 1)×100`\n\n## CRITICAL NEXT STEP\n\n**The user MUST run Excel Solver to complete the HP filter:**\n\n1. Open `test-supply.xlsx`\n2. Go to `Production` sheet\n3. Data → Solver (enable add-in if needed)\n4. Set Objective: N10\n5. To: Min\n6. By Changing: H9:H27\n7. Solving Method: GRG Nonlinear\n8. Click Solve → OK\n\nThis smooths the TFP series and enables accurate potential GDP estimation.\n\n## Results Location\n\nAfter running Solver, view results in Production sheet:\n- **Columns D & I**: Capital stock (baseline vs with shock)\n- **Columns F & J**: Potential GDP (baseline vs with shock)\n- **Column K**: Absolute GDP impact (billions USD)\n- **Column L**: Percentage GDP impact (%)\n- **Focus years**: 2026-2041 for shock impact analysis\n\n## Investment Shock Details\n\n- **Total amount**: $6.5 billion USD\n- **Duration**: 8 years (2026-2033)\n- **Annual amount**: $0.8125 billion/year\n- **Distribution**: Equal annual installments\n- **Impact**: Increases capital stock, which raises potential GDP through production function\n\n## Data Quality Notes\n\n1. **PWT data** ends in 2019 - post-2019 capital stock is projected\n2. **Growth forecasts** beyond 2027 assume constant 5% (IMF medium-term potential)\n3. **Depreciation rate** averaged over 2016-2023 to smooth fluctuations\n4. **K/Y ratio** anchored to 2015-2023 average for stability\n\n## Technical Specifications\n\n- **Model type**: Cobb-Douglas production function\n- **TFP extraction**: Hodrick-Prescott filter (λ=100)\n- **Time horizon**: 2002-2041 (40 years)\n- **Shock period**: 2026-2033 (8 years)\n- **Projection period**: 2024-2041 (18 years)\n\n## Files Generated\n\n1. **test-supply.xlsx** - Main model file with all data and formulas\n2. **MODEL_README.md** - This documentation file\n\n## Assumptions\n\n1. Baseline investment maintains K/Y ratio\n5. No labor constraint (small open economy assumption)\n6. Projected: `=(1-δ)×K_{t-1} + (Y_t×(K/Y)_anchor - (1-δ)×K_{t-1})`\n\n4. **Potential GDP** (Production, F36-F75):\n   - `=EXP(LnZ_trend)×K^α`\n\n5. **Impact Calculation** (Production, K36-L75):\n   - Absolute: `=Y*_with - Y*_base`\n   - Percentage: `=(Y*_with/Y*_base - 1)×100`\n\n## CRITICAL NEXT STEP\n\n**The user MUST run Excel Solver to complete the HP filter:**\n\n1.
